of the same length have the same extension class if and only if there exists a periodic quasi-isomorphism between them.

Proof.

Let
\upvartheta\in\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)
be the class of the periodic projective resolution

0\to M\xrightarrow{\upbeta}P_{n-1}\xrightarrow{d_{n-1}}P_{n-2}\to\dots\to P_{1}\xrightarrow{d_{1}}P_{0}\xrightarrow{\upalpha}M\to 0.

(4.B)

Following
[
Y
]
, there is a natural map from the set of
n
-fold extensions
E^{n}(M,M)
to
\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)
. To describe this, first choose a projective resolution of
M
, truncate it at length
n
to obtain

0\to\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits(\updelta_{n-1})\xrightarrow{\upiota}E_{n-1}\xrightarrow{\updelta_{n-1}}E_{n-2}\to\dots\to E_{1}\xrightarrow{\updelta_{1}}E_{0}\xrightarrow{\upvarepsilon}M\to 0,

then choose a chain map
f
that lifts the identity on
M
, as follows

0
\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits(\updelta_{n-1})
E_{n-1}
E_{n-2}
\ldots
E_{1}
E_{0}
M
0
\scriptstyle\upiota
\scriptstyle\upvarepsilon
0
M
P_{n-1}
P_{n-2}
\ldots
P_{1}
P_{0}
M
0
\scriptstyle\upbeta
\scriptstyle\upalpha
\scriptstyle f_{n}
\scriptstyle f_{n-1}
\scriptstyle f_{n-2}
\scriptstyle f_{1}
\scriptstyle f_{0}

Such a chain map always exists and it is unique up to homotopy (see e.g.
4.1
). Then the Yoneda map
[
Y
]
is

\upchi\colon E^{n}(M,M)\to\tfrac{\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits(\updelta_{n-1}),M)}{\circ\upiota(\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(E_{n-1},M))}\cong\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M),

(4.C)

defined by sending
[0\to M\to P_{n-1}\to\ldots\to P_{0}\to M]\mapsto[f_{n}]
.
The map
\upchi
is an isomorphism between
n
-fold extensions modulo equivalence and
\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M).

In our case it is natural to choose as a truncated projective resolution the exact sequence (
4.B
), and the chain map
f
to be the identity. Therefore the class
\upvartheta\in\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)
corresponds to the class of the identity map in
\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)/{\circ\upbeta(\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(P_{n-1},M))}
.

Consider now another periodic projective resolution of length
n
of
M
, namely

0\to M\xrightarrow{\upbeta^{\prime}}P^{\prime}_{n-1}\xrightarrow{d^{\prime}_{n-1}}P^{\prime}_{n-2}\to\dots\to P^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{d^{\prime}_{1}}P^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\upalpha^{\prime}}M\to 0,

with extension class
\upvartheta^{\prime}
. There exists a map
g
of complexes

0
M
P_{n-1}
P_{n-2}
\ldots
P_{1}
P_{0}
M
0
\scriptstyle\upbeta
\scriptstyle\upalpha
0
M
P^{\prime}_{n-1}
P^{\prime}_{n-2}
\ldots
P^{\prime}_{1}
P^{\prime}_{0}
M
0
\scriptstyle\upbeta^{\prime}
\scriptstyle\upalpha^{\prime}
\scriptstyle g_{n}
\scriptstyle g_{n-1}
\scriptstyle g_{n-2}
\scriptstyle g_{1}
\scriptstyle g_{0}

such that
[g_{n}]=\upvartheta^{\prime}
. Then by
[
Y
, 3.5]

\upvartheta=\upvartheta^{\prime}\iff[g_{n}]=[\operatorname{Id}_{M}]\iff g_{n}-\operatorname{Id}_{M}\in\operatorname{Im}(\circ\upbeta).

Now, suppose that
g_{n}-\operatorname{Id}_{M}\in\operatorname{Im}(\circ\upbeta)
, so there exists
h\in\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(P_{n-1},M)
such that
g_{n}-\operatorname{Id}_{M}=h\circ\upbeta
. Then consider the map
\mathsf{g}\colon\mathrm{P}\to\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
defined by

\mathsf{g_{i}}=\begin{cases}g_{i}&\mbox{ if }0\leq i\leq n-2,\\
g_{n-1}-\upbeta^{\prime}\circ h&\mbox{ if }i=n-1.\end{cases}

Since

\mathsf{g}_{n-1}\circ\upbeta={g_{n-1}}\circ\upbeta-\upbeta^{\prime}\circ h\circ\upbeta=\upbeta^{\prime}\circ g_{n}-\upbeta^{\prime}\circ g_{n}+\upbeta^{\prime}=\upbeta^{\prime},

\mathsf{g}
is a periodic quasi-isomorphism. The converse is clear, by
4.3
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

A periodic projective resolution
\mathrm{P}
of
M
determines a surjective map

\uppi\colon\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)\twoheadrightarrow\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M),

given by the Yoneda product
(-)\smile\upvartheta
, where
\upvartheta
is the extension class of the periodic projective resolution. In particular, if
\upvartheta=0
then
\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)=0
.

Proof.

There is a natural surjection
\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)\twoheadrightarrow\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)/{\circ\upbeta(\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(P_{n-1},M))}
. The claim is, once we identify the right hand side with
E^{n}(M,M)
modulo equivalence as in the proof of
4.4
, that this map is given by the Yoneda product with
\upvartheta
.

This follows directly from the definition of the Yoneda product
g\smile\upvartheta
for
g\in\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)
, which we recall is defined as the extension class of the second exact sequence in the diagram below:

0
M
P_{n-1}
P_{n-2}
\ldots
P_{1}
P_{0}
M
0
\scriptstyle\upbeta
\scriptstyle d_{n-1}
\scriptstyle\upalpha
0
M
M\oplus_{g}P_{n-1}
P_{n-2}
\ldots
P_{1}
P_{0}
M
0
\scriptstyle(0,d_{n-1})
\scriptstyle\upalpha^{\prime}
\scriptstyle g

where
M\oplus_{g}P_{n-1}
is the pushout along
g
and
\upbeta
, namely

M\oplus P_{n-1}/{\{g(n)-\upbeta(n)\ |\ n\in M\}}.

Since
g\smile\upvartheta
is the second sequence, it follows that
\upchi
sends
g\smile\upvartheta
to
[g]
, and thus

\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)
\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)/\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits(\circ\upbeta)
E^{n}(M,M)/\sim
\scriptstyle(-)\smile\upvartheta
\scriptstyle\upchi

commutes, as claimed.

Now
\upvartheta=0
in
\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)
if and only if
\operatorname{Id}_{M}
belongs to
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits(\circ\upbeta)
, which is if and only if
\operatorname{Id}_{M}=h\circ\upbeta
for some
h\colon P_{n-1}\to M
. In that case, for any
g\in\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(M,M)
,
g=g\circ\operatorname{Id}_{M}=g\circ h\circ\upbeta
, so that
g\in\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits(\circ\upbeta)
and hence it is zero in the
Ext
group. Since
\uppi
is a surjection, this implies that
\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(M,M)=0
.
∎

The following special case will be used in future sections.

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
\mathrm{P},\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
be two periodic projective resolutions of length
n
of a simple
\Upgamma
-module
S
. Then for the associated extension classes
\upvartheta,\upvartheta^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(S,S)
, either
\upvartheta=\upvartheta^{\prime}=0
or
\upvartheta^{\prime}=\uplambda\upvartheta
for some
\uplambda\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

Proof.

By
4.5
, there is a surjection
\mathbb{C}\cong\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(S,S)\twoheadrightarrow\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(S,S)
. It follows that
\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(S,S)
is either
\mathbb{C}
or zero. Write
\upvartheta,\upvartheta^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Ext}^{n}_{\Upgamma}(S,S)
for the extension class associated to
\mathrm{P}
and
\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
respectively. If
\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(S,S)=0
, then
\upvartheta=\upvartheta^{\prime}=0
.
The other case is when
\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(S,S)\cong\mathbb{C}
, in which case
\upvartheta^{\prime}=\uplambda\upvartheta
for some
\uplambda\in\mathbb{C}
. If
\uplambda=0
, then
\upvartheta^{\prime}=0
, so by
4.5
necessarily
\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(S,S)=0
, which is a contradiction. Thus
\upvartheta^{\prime}=\uplambda\upvartheta
with
\uplambda\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.
∎

5.
Strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-algebras

In this section, we recall some definitions and technical results about strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-algebras, mainly following
[
LH
]
.

Definition 5.1
.

An
A_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathcal{A},\{m_{i}\}_{i\geq 1})
is
strictly unital
if it is equipped with an element of degree zero
\upeta_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
m_{2}(a\otimes\,\upeta_{\mathcal{A}})=m_{2}(\upeta_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes a)=a
for all
a\in\mathcal{A}
, and for all
i\neq 2
,

m_{i}(a_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes a_{j-1}\otimes\,\upeta_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes a_{j+1}\otimes\ldots\otimes a_{i})=0

for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,i\}
and all
a_{1},\ldots,a_{j-1},a_{j+1},\ldots,a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
.

A
DG
-algebra with unity, considered as an
A_{\infty}
-algebra, is strictly unital.

Given an
A_{\infty}
-morphism
f\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
, we write
f_{i}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\otimes i}\to\mathcal{B}
for its components, which are of degree
1-i
.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}
be two strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-algebras. An
A_{\infty}
-morphism
f\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
is
strictly unital
if
f_{1}(\upeta_{\mathcal{A}})=\upeta_{\mathcal{B}}
and for every
i\neq 1
,

f_{i}(a_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes a_{j-1}\otimes\,\upeta_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes a_{j+1}\otimes\ldots\otimes a_{i})=0

for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,i\}
and all
a_{1},\ldots,a_{j-1},a_{j+1},\ldots,a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
.

It is clear that composition of strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-morphisms is again a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-morphism. Trivially, any unit-preserving
DG
-algebra morphism of unital
DG
-algebras is a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-morphism.

Kadeishvili’s Theorem, known more generally as the Homotopy Transfer Theorem, equips the cohomology of a
DG
-algebra with an
A_{\infty}
-structure, which is unique up to quasi-isomorphism
[
K1
]
. We are interested here in the strictly unital version of this theorem, or to be precise the following strictly unital version of the improved result due to Markl
[
M
]
.

Proposition 5.3

(
[
LH
, 3.2.4.1, 3.2.4.2]
)
.

Any strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
satisfying
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\neq 0
admits a strictly unital minimal model
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
such that the
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms

i\colon\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A},\quad p\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}

are strictly unital.

This implies that given a unital
DG
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
with non-trivial cohomology, its cohomology
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
can be equipped with a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-structure
(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{m_{i}\}_{i\geq 2})
such that the extensions to
A_{\infty}
-morphisms of the inclusion
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{A}
and projection
\mathcal{A}\to\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
(note that the inclusion and projection maps are not unique), are
both
strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms.

Assumption 5.4
.

In the following, we will always assume that
DG
-algebras
\mathcal{A}
are unital and that the identity is not a coboundary, hence
\mathsf{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{A}}]\in\textnormal{H}^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is non-zero.

For a unital
DG
-algebra (or for a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-algebra), the identity is easily seen to be non-trivial in cohomology if and only if the cohomology is non-trivial.
This means that we can apply
5.3
to any such
DG
-algebra.

Lemma 5.5
.

Given a unital
DG
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
satisfying
5.4
, the induced strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-structure on its cohomology restricts to an
A_{\infty}
-structure on
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a unital
DG
-algebra, and let
i\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{A}
be a morphism of complexes inducing the identity in cohomology, and such that
i(\mathsf{1})=\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
p\colon\mathcal{A}\to\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
be a unit-preserving (so
p(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{A}})=\mathsf{1}
) morphism of complexes such that
p\circ i=\operatorname{Id}
. By
5.3
, there exists a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-structure
(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{m_{i}\}_{i\geq 2})
such that
i,p
can be extended to strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms.

We claim that for degree reasons, this
A_{\infty}
-structure restricts to an
A_{\infty}
-structure on
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
. First, recall that
m_{2}(a_{1},a_{2})
lives in degree
|a_{1}|+|a_{2}|
, which for inputs in
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
is always strictly greater than zero unless
|a_{1}|=|a_{2}|=0
. But in that case
a_{1}=\uplambda\mathsf{1},a_{2}=\upmu\mathsf{1}
for some
\uplambda,\upmu\in\mathbb{C}
, so
m_{2}(\uplambda\mathsf{1},\upmu\mathsf{1})=\uplambda\upmu\mathsf{1}\in\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
. Thus always
m_{2}
restricts to
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
. For the higher products, for inputs in
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
by strict unitality
m_{i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{i})
is non-zero only if
|a_{j}|\geq 1
for all
1\leq j\leq i
, and in that case

|m_{i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{i})|=\sum_{j=1}^{i}|a_{j}|+2-i\geq i+2-i=2.\qed

Definition 5.6
.

Given a unital
DG
-algebra
\mathcal{B}
satisfying
5.4
, we call the
A_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}=\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{B})
equipped with the
A_{\infty}
-structure given by
5.5
, the
unitally positive
A_{\infty}
-algebra
associated to
\mathcal{B}
.

We next show that this new object is well-defined.

Proposition 5.7
.

If two unital
DG
-algebras
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
satisfying
5.4
are quasi-isomorphic as unital
DG
-algebras, then there exists an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be two unital
DG
-algebras which are quasi-isomorphic as unital
DG
-algebras, i.e., such that they are connected by a zigzag of quasi-isomorphisms of unital
DG
-algebras which are unit-preserving. It is well known that one can replace a zigzag of
DG
-algebra quasi-isomorphisms with an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
, see e.g.
[
LV
, 11.4.9]
, but we could not find a strictly unital version of this result, so we prove it for completeness.
Let

\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{0}\xleftarrow{f_{1}}\mathcal{A}_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{2}}\mathcal{A}_{2}\xleftarrow{}\ldots\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{A}_{n}=\mathcal{B}

be a zigzag of quasi-isomorphisms of
DG
-algebras, such that for every
j
,
\mathcal{A}_{j}
is unital and
f_{j}
is unit-preserving. Without loss of generality, we show that we can replace
f_{1}
with a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism in the opposite direction.
Denote by
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{i})
the strictly unital minimal model of
\mathcal{A}_{i}
obtained by
5.3
, and by
i\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{j})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}_{j}
,
p\colon\mathcal{A}_{j}\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{j})
the
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms. The composition

p\circ f_{1}\circ i\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})

is a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebras, so it is a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism. Then by
[
LH
, 3.2.4.6]
its
A_{\infty}
-inverse
\upbeta\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1})
is also strictly unital, and the composition

i\circ\upbeta\circ p\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}_{1}

is a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism. Proceeding in this way for all the maps in the zigzag which point left, we obtain a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
f\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
.

We next claim that the strict unitality implies that the composition

p\circ f\circ i\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{B})
, which is a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism,
restricts to an
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism between
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
and
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{B})
. In fact, for every
n>1
, if some
x_{i}
is of degree zero then
f_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=0
. Else, when applied to inputs in
\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathcal{A})
,

|f_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|x_{i}|+1-n\geq n+1-n=1.

The linear component
f_{1}
is of degree zero, so we just need to check that
f_{1}(\mathsf{1})=\mathsf{1}
, which follows by strict unitality. Combining, this proves the claim. Thus since
f_{1}
(which has degree zero) is still an isomorphism after restriction, the result follows.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

The construction of
\mathcal{N}
can be seen to define a right adjoint to the inclusion
(\rm{HoDGA})^{\geq 1}\hookrightarrow\rm{HoDGA}
of the full subcategory of DG-algebras
\mathcal{A}
such that
\textnormal{H}^{0}(\mathcal{A})=\mathbb{C}
and
\textnormal{H}^{<0}(\mathcal{A})=0
into the homotopy category of DG-algebras satisfying
5.4
.

An analogue result, at the level of
A_{\infty}
-algebras (i.e., not on the homotopy category), is shown in
[
R
, 6.3]
, while the last part of the proof of
5.7
follows from 6.2 of
loc. cit
.

6.
The Case of Idempotents

In this section consider the setting where
\mathrm{A}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra with idempotent
e\in\mathrm{A}
. Set
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{A}/\mathrm{A}e\mathrm{A}
, then the ring homomorphism
\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
induces the restriction and extension of scalars adjunction

\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}._{\phantom{con}}
\scriptstyle i_{*}
\scriptstyle i^{*}

Setup 6.1
.

Given a finite-dimensional simple module
S\in\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}
, suppose that there exists a complex of finitely generated projective
\mathrm{A}
-modules

\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\quad 0\rightarrow Q_{n-1}\rightarrow Q_{n-2}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{0}\rightarrow 0

which gives a
\mathrm{A}
-projective resolution of
S
, such that
\mathrm{P}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}i^{*}\mathcal{Q}
satisfies

\mathrm{H}^{-j}(\mathrm{P})\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i^{*}S&\text{ if $j\in\{0,n-1\}$ }\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

As such, we may choose
\upalpha
and
\upbeta
such that

0\to i^{*}S\xrightarrow{\upbeta}P_{n-1}\to P_{n-2}\to\ldots\to P_{1}\to P_{0}\xrightarrow{\upalpha}i^{*}S\to 0

is exact, and thus induce a length
n
periodic projective resolution of
i^{*}S
. Using Setup
2.1
, and
2.11
, we thus obtain a trivial extension
DG
-algebra
\mathcal{T}
, and the quasi-isomorphic
DG
-algebra
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})
.

Notation 6.2
.

We will often abuse notation and simply write
S
for
i^{*}(S)
, that is,
S
viewed as an
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-module. This is justified, since the subscript on the Ext groups below will always tell us in which category
S
should be viewed.

Remark 6.3
.

Since
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}},S)\cong S
, and in Setup
6.1

S
is assumed to be finite dimensional, it follows that
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(P,S)
is finite dimensional for all finitely generated projective
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-modules
P
. In particular, the following statements hold.

(1)

In the notation of
3.7
and
3.8
, necessarily
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits(\circ d_{0})\subseteq\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(P_{0},S)
is finite dimensional.

(2)

For
i=1,\ldots,n-1
, each
\textnormal{Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S,S)
is finite dimensional, given that it can be computed as a quotient of a subspace of
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(P_{i},S)
.

6.1.
Uniqueness for simples

It is well-known, see e.g.
[
ST
, 4.4]
, that given another
\mathrm{A}
-projective resolution
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
of
S
, the
DG
-algebras
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
are quasi-isomorphic. However, the situation we will be interested in is the one of two rings
\mathrm{A},\mathrm{B}
with idempotents such that
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
. Thus, we will need to compare two periodic projective resolutions of length
n
of
S
coming respectively from an
\mathrm{A}
-projective resolution
\mathcal{Q}
of length
n
of
S
and an
\mathrm{B}
-projective
resolution
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
of length
n
of
S
.

This turns out to be possible using the fact that the module is simple, via
4.6
. The following works more generally.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\Upgamma
be a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra,
S
a simple
\Upgamma
-module, and let
\mathrm{P},\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
be two periodic projective resolutions of length
n
of
S
, as in (
4.A
).
Then there exists a quasi-isomorphism between
\mathrm{P}
and
\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
, so
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime})
are quasi-isomorphic as unital
DG
-algebras.

Proof.

Writing
\upvartheta,\upvartheta^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Ext}_{\Upgamma}^{n}(S,S)
for the extension classes of
\mathrm{P}
and
\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
, then by
4.6
either
\upvartheta=\upvartheta^{\prime}=0
or
\upvartheta^{\prime}=\uplambda\upvartheta
for some
\uplambda\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

If
\upvartheta=\upvartheta^{\prime}=0
then by
4.4

\mathrm{P}
and
\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
are linked by a periodic quasi-isomorphism, so that by
[
ST
, 4.4]
the
DG
-algebras
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime})
are quasi-isomorphic. Notice that the proof of
[
ST
, 4.4]
works by constructing a roof of unit-preserving quasi-isomorphisms of unital
DG
-algebras
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P})\leftarrow N\to\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime})
, so that
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime})
are quasi-isomorphic as unital
DG
-algebras.

If
\upvartheta^{\prime}=\uplambda\upvartheta
with
\uplambda\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, there exists a chain map
g

0
S
P_{n-1}
P_{n-2}
\ldots
P_{1}
P_{0}
S
0
\scriptstyle\upbeta
\scriptstyle d_{n-1}
\scriptstyle\upalpha
0
S
P^{\prime}_{n-1}
P^{\prime}_{n-2}
\ldots
P^{\prime}_{1}
P^{\prime}_{0}
S
0
\scriptstyle\upbeta^{\prime}
\scriptstyle\upalpha^{\prime}
\scriptstyle g_{n}
\scriptstyle g_{n-1}
\scriptstyle g_{n-2}
\scriptstyle g_{1}
\scriptstyle g_{0}

such that
g_{n}-\uplambda\operatorname{Id}_{S}=h\circ\upbeta
, for some
h\in\textnormal{Hom}_{\Upgamma}(P_{n-1},S)
. As in the proof of
4.4
, we now modify the map
g
. Consider
\mathsf{g}\colon\mathrm{P}\to\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
defined by

\mathsf{g}_{i}=\begin{cases}g_{i}&\mbox{if }i=0,\ldots,n-2\\
g_{n-1}-\upbeta^{\prime}\circ h&\mbox{if }i=n-1.\end{cases}

It is easy to see that

\mathsf{g}_{n-1}\circ\upbeta={g_{n-1}}\circ\upbeta-\upbeta^{\prime}\circ h\circ\upbeta=\upbeta^{\prime}\circ g_{-n}-\upbeta^{\prime}\circ h\circ\upbeta=\uplambda\upbeta^{\prime}.

From this,
\mathsf{g}
is easily seen to be a quasi-isomorphism and therefore the
DG
-algebras of endomorphisms are again quasi-isomorphic by
[
ST
, 4.4]
.
∎

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
\Upgamma
be a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra,
S\in\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\Upgamma
be simple, and let
\mathrm{P},\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
be two periodic projective resolutions of the simple module
S
, of the same length. Then the strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-algebras
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P}))
and
\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}))
are quasi-isomorphic.

Proof.

Using the fact that
S
is simple,
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime})
are quasi-isomorphic as unital
DG
-algebras by
6.4
. The conclusion then follows from the general
5.7
.
∎

6.2.
Main reconstruction result

Under Setup
6.1
, since
\mathrm{P}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}i^{*}\mathcal{Q}
, consider the morphism

f\in\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})\to i^{*}f\in\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}).

It is clear that
\updelta(i^{*}f)=i^{*}\updelta(f)
, and that the above is a morphism of unital
DG
-algebras.

This is not a quasi-isomorphism, but it will become one once we pass to the unitally positive
A_{\infty}
-algebra. The point will then be (in
6.7
) that
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})
, which is built on the large ring
\mathrm{A}
, can be recovered from the isomorphism class of the smaller ring
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
. The following is our main technical result.

Theorem 6.6
.

Under Setup
6.1
, there is an
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism between the strictly unital minimal model of
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})
and
{\mathcal{N}=\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}))}
.

Proof.

Let
\upvarepsilon\colon\mathcal{Q}\to S
be the
\mathrm{A}
-projective resolution of
S
of length
n
in Setup
6.1
, and define
\upalpha
as the map
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}(\mathcal{Q})=\mathrm{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}(\upvarepsilon)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upalpha}

where
\upiota\colon\mathrm{P}\to\mathcal{P}
is the inclusion as in §
3.1
.

Consider the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P},\mathcal{P})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q},S)\cong\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P},S)},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upvarepsilon\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upiota\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upalpha\circ}

(6.A)

which commutes because
\upalpha\circ\upiota=i^{*}(\upvarepsilon)
. The maps
\upvarepsilon\colon\mathcal{Q}\to S
and
\upalpha\colon\mathcal{P}\to S
are projective resolutions, hence the maps
\upvarepsilon\circ
and
\upalpha\circ
in the diagram above are quasi-isomorphisms of complexes. In fact, the cone of
\upvarepsilon\circ
is isomorphic to
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q},\operatorname{cone}(\upvarepsilon))
, which is acyclic, because any morphism of complexes from a bounded-above complex of projective modules to an acyclic complex is homotopic to the zero map (see e.g.
[
GM
, III.5.24]
), and analogously for
\upalpha\circ
.

This implies that we can calculate the cohomology of
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})
as a graded vector space using (
3.E
), via

\displaystyle\textnormal{Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}}(S,S)

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{H}^{i}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}))

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{H}^{i}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P},\mathcal{P}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\quad i<0,\ i\geq n\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S,S)&\quad i=0,\ldots,n-2\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S,S)\oplus\operatorname{Im}(\circ d_{0})&\quad i=n-1.\end{cases}

(6.B)

Using the diagram (
6.A
) we can relate the cohomology of
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})
, i.e.,
\textnormal{Ext}^{*}_{\mathrm{A}}(S,S)
, to the cohomology of
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})
, which was calculated in §
3.3
. From the commutativity of the diagram (
6.A
) and the fact that
\upvarepsilon\circ,\upalpha\circ
are quasi-isomorphisms, the composition along the top line of (
6.A
) is a quasi-isomorphism. Thus
i^{*}\colon\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})\to\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})
is injective in cohomology, and
\upiota\circ\colon\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})\to\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P},\mathcal{P})
is surjective in cohomology.

Now consider the unitally positive
A_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathcal{N}=\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}))
defined in
5.6
. From
3.8
we have

\mathcal{N}^{i}=\begin{cases}0&\quad i\leq-1,\ i\geq n\\
\mathbb{C}&\quad i=0\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S,S)&\quad i=1,\ldots,n-2\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S,S)\oplus\operatorname{Im}(\circ d_{0})&\quad i=n-1.\end{cases}

(6.C)

Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}))}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

By
5.3
, the projection
p
and the inclusion
j
can be extended to strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms, so we obtain a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-morphism

\upvarphi\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}))\rightsquigarrow\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})\xrightarrow{i^{*}}\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})),

with linear component
\upvarphi_{1}=p\circ i^{*}\circ j
. The linear component
\upvarphi_{1}
of the
A_{\infty}
-morphism
\upvarphi
can be thought of as the map induced by
i^{*}
in cohomology: hence, as remarked above, it is injective.

By the cohomology calculation (
6.B
) and by the same argument as in
5.7
, since
\upvarphi
is strictly unital, a simple degree calculation shows that all its components have image contained in
\mathcal{N}
, so there is an
A_{\infty}
-morphism

\upvarphi\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}))\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}=\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})),

(6.D)

with linear component
\upvarphi_{1}=p\circ i^{*}\circ j
, which is injective.

Now by
6.3
all the vector spaces in (
6.C
) are finite dimensional, and any injective linear map between vector spaces of the same dimension is an isomorphism. Thus, now by simply comparing the cohomology of
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})
in (
6.B
) with
\mathcal{N}^{i}
in (
6.C
), the injective map

\upvarphi_{1}\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}))\to\mathcal{N}=\mathbb{C}\mathsf{1}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}))

is necessarily an isomorphism, which by definition implies that
\upvarphi
in (
6.D
) is an
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism.
∎

The following shows that under Setup
6.1
, the strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-minimal model of
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q})
can be reconstructed from the isomorphism class of
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
.

Corollary 6.7
.

Suppose that both
\mathrm{A}
and
\mathrm{B}
admit simples and idempotents which satisfy the conditions in Setup
6.1
, via complexes
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{A}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{B}}
of the same length. If
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
and both are local, then there is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{A}})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{B}})
.

Proof.

The simple
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-module
S
is equipped with a length
n
complex
\mathrm{P}
which builds
\mathcal{N}_{S}
, and by
6.6
there is an
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism

\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{A}}))\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}_{S}

where the left hand side is equipped with the
A_{\infty}
-structure of a strictly unital minimal model. The same analysis holds for
\mathrm{B}
, where
\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
has simple
S^{\prime}
which is equipped with a complex
\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
of the same length
n
which in turn builds
\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}}
. By
6.6
, there is an
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism

\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{B}}))\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}}.

It suffices to prove that
\mathcal{N}_{S}
and
\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}}
are
A_{\infty}
-isomorphic, and for this we will ultimately use
5.7
.
Indeed, since
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
, there is an (exact) isomorphism of categories
F\colon\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\to\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
. Now
F
preserves simples, so since both
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
and
\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
are local,
FS\cong S^{\prime}
. Further,
F
takes
\mathrm{P}
to some length
n
complex of projectives which builds a periodic projective resolution of
S^{\prime}
. Clearly this isomorphism of categories induces a
DG
-algebra isomorphism (not just quasi-isomorphism!)

F\colon\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})\to\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(F\mathrm{P})

(6.E)

which preserves the identity. Now
F\mathrm{P}
is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathrm{P}^{\prime}
by
6.4
, as both give periodic resolutions of the same simple with the same length, and so again by
4.1
(
3
)
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(F\mathrm{P})
is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime})
. Combining with (
6.E
), it follows that the unital
DG
-algebras
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime})
are quasi-isomorphic. By
6.5
,
\mathcal{N}_{S}
and
\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}}
are
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphic, proving the result.
∎

7.
Flops

Throughout, let
X\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R
be a
3
-fold flopping contraction, where
X
is smooth and
R
is complete local. Here, we continue to work over the complex numbers.

7.1.
Notation

As is well-known
[
V
]
,
X
admits a tilting bundle
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{M}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{M}_{t}
, where the summands
\mathcal{M}_{i}
are in bijection with the exceptional curves
C_{1},\ldots,C_{t}
. Each
C_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Writing
\mathrm{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\textnormal{End}_{X}(\mathcal{V})\cong\textnormal{End}_{R}(f_{*}\mathcal{V})
, then
\mathrm{A}
is a noncommutative crepant resolution (NCCR), and there is a derived equivalence

\mathop{\rm{D}^{b}}\nolimits(\mathop{\textnormal{coh}}\nolimits X)\xrightarrow{\textnormal{RHom}_{X}(\mathcal{V},-)}\mathop{\rm{D}^{b}}\nolimits(\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}).

Since
R
is complete local, the category
\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}
has finitely many simples, which geometrically are described in
[
V
, 3.5.7, 3.5.8]
. Matching the notation in
[
V
]
, we will write
S_{1},\ldots,S_{t}
for the simple
\mathrm{A}
-modules that are in bijection with the exceptional curves
C_{1},\ldots,C_{t}
. Each
S_{i}\cong\mathbb{C}
, and thus is finite dimensional.

Let
e
be the idempotent of
\mathrm{A}
corresponding to the summand
R=f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
of
f_{*}\mathcal{V}
. Then by
[
DW1
]
the contraction algebra
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
can be defined simply as

\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{A}/\mathrm{A}e\mathrm{A}.

Since
f
is a flopping contraction,
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is finite dimensional
[
DW1
, 2.13]
, and using the same mild abuse of notation as in
6.2
, the simple
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-modules are
S_{1},\ldots,S_{t}
.

Since
\mathrm{A}
is an NCCR, by Auslander–Buchsbaum
[
IR
, 2.3]
each
S_{i}\in\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}
has projective dimension three, so consider the minimal projective resolution
\mathcal{Q}^{j}\to S_{j}\to 0
. Applying
i^{*}=-\otimes_{\mathrm{A}}\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
gives a complex of projective
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-modules

\mathrm{P}^{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\quad P^{j}_{3}\to P^{j}_{2}\to P^{j}_{1}\to P^{j}_{0},

(7.A)

whose cohomology computes
\textnormal{Tor}_{*}^{\mathrm{A}}(S_{j},\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}})
. The following is a well-known consequence of the fact that
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is spherical.

Lemma 7.1
.

In this flops setting, for every
j=1,\ldots,t
,

\textnormal{Tor}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}}(S_{j},\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}})\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{C}&\text{ if $k\in\{0,3\}$ }\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Since
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is a symmetric finite dimensional algebra
[
A
, 3.3]
,
D\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
as bimodules, where
D
is the
\mathbb{C}
-dual. Thus

\displaystyle D\textnormal{Tor}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}}(S_{j},\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}})

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}}(S_{j},D\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}})

(by
[
CE
, VI.5.1]
)

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}}(S_{j},\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}})

(
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is symmetric)

\displaystyle\cong D\textnormal{Ext}^{3-k}_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}},S_{j}).

(
\mathrm{A}
is 3-CY)

\displaystyle\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D\,\mathbb{C}&\text{ if $k\in\{0,3\}$ }\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

(
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is spherical
[
DW3
, 4.7]
)

The result follows, by cancelling the duality
D
.
∎

Since
i^{*}
is right exact, the cokernel of the rightmost map in (
7.A
) is
S_{j}
. In particular, by
7.1
there is an exact sequence of
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-modules

0\to\Omega^{4}S_{j}\to P^{j}_{3}\to P^{j}_{2}\to P^{j}_{1}\to P^{j}_{0}\xrightarrow{\upalpha}S_{j}\to 0.

Since contraction algebras are four-periodic
[
D
, 1.1]
, necessarily the module
\Omega^{4}S_{j}
, which is one-dimensional by
7.1
, is isomorphic to
S_{j}
. Passing through this isomorphism gives an exact sequence of
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-modules

0\to S_{j}\xrightarrow{\upbeta}P^{j}_{3}\to P^{j}_{2}\to P^{j}_{1}\to P^{j}_{0}\xrightarrow{\upalpha}S_{j}\to 0

for each
j
such that
1\leq j\leq t
.

We now consider the sum of the simples
S=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{t}S_{j}
, the sum of the projective
\mathrm{A}
-resolutions
\mathcal{Q}_{S}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{t}\mathcal{Q}^{j}
, and the sum
\mathrm{P}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{t}\mathrm{P}^{j}
. Thus
S
is finite dimensional, and there is a projective resolution
\mathcal{Q}_{S}\to S
such that
\mathrm{P}=i^{*}\mathcal{Q}_{S}
satisfies

\mathrm{H}^{-j}(\mathrm{P})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}S&\text{ if $j\in\{0,3\}$ }\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

(7.B)

7.2.
Single curve Donovan–Wemyss

The case when there is a single curve, namely
t=1
, now follows easily since the
S
in (
7.B
) is both finite dimensional and simple, and so (
7.B
) and the paragraphs above fall directly within the remit of Setup
6.1
.

Corollary 7.2

(Conjecture
[
DW1
, 1.4]
)
.

Suppose that
X_{1}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{1}
and
X_{2}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{2}
are two
3
-fold flopping contractions, where both
X_{i}
are smooth, both
R_{i}
are complete local, and both contractions have precisely one curve above the origin. Write
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
and
\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
for their corresponding contraction algebras. Then

R_{1}\cong R_{2}\iff\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}.

Proof.

The implication (1)
\Rightarrow
(2) is contained in the original paper
[
DW1
]
, the content is the direction (2)
\Rightarrow
(1). In addition to notation already introduced, write
\mathrm{A}
and
\mathrm{B}
for the two NCCRs. Since for both contractions the corresponding (
7.B
) falls within the remit of Setup
6.1
, we can simply quote
6.7
to conclude that there must be an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{A}})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{B}})
.

From here, the proof is standard. As a consequence of the above, the Koszul duals
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{A}})^{!}
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{B}})^{!}
are
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphic. But these are the derived contraction algebras of Booth
[
B
]
(see also
[
KY
, 2.6, 2.7]
), and so the derived contraction algebras are
DG
-quasi-isomorphic (see e.g.
[
L
, 2.8]
or
[
LV
, 11.4.9]
). The result then follows directly from
[
B
, 8.3.3]
, since a quasi-isomorphism between the derived contraction algebras implies that the relative and classical singularity categories are equivalent, and so we can use the recovery theorem of
[
HK
, 5.9]
.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

Although the proof of
7.2
does not split into cases, this is only since the two cases in the proof of
6.4
both led to the same outcome. In reality, there are two cases: when
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is semisimple (namely
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathbb{C}
, the ‘Atiyah flop’) and otherwise. In the Atiyah case the complex
\mathrm{P}
is

\mathbb{C}\to 0\to 0\to\mathbb{C}

whereas in all other cases
\mathrm{P}
has four non-zero terms, and the morphisms all lie in the radical.
Regardless, in both cases we can appeal to
6.4
, and this is what makes a uniform proof of
7.2
possible.

The multi-curve version
t>1
of the conjecture is proved in
8.13
below, but to do this requires some constructions to be extended so that they work over a semi-simple base. Indeed, when there is more than one curve, there is more than one simple, so the
S
in (
7.B
) is semi-simple (not simple) and thus
6.7
is not directly applicable. However, the extension needed in Section
8
below is very mild, and the main ideas remain the same.

8.
Categorical Upgrade

The purpose of this section is to first extend (in §
8.1
) the notion of ‘unitally positive’ from
A_{\infty}
-algebras to
A_{\infty}
-categories, then (in §
8.2
) to use this to extend the results in §
6
to cover semi-simple modules. Once this is done, in §
8.3
the multi-curve generalisation of
7.2
easily follows.

In this section, we remark that instead of the more widely used notion of quasi-equivalence of
A_{\infty}
-categories, we will instead use the notion of quasi-isomorphism,
i.e., a quasi-equivalence such that the map between the sets of objects is a bijection, see e.g.
[
COS2
, 1.13]
. We will mostly consider
A_{\infty}
-categories with a finite number of objects, and so the objects form a set. Thus we can appeal to
[
LH
, Section 5.1]
, and so the results of Section
5
will generalise easily, provided that when we consider strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-categories we require the property that for all
x\in\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}
, the complex
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,x)
has non-trivial cohomology.

8.1.
Unitally positive categories

Definition 8.1
.

A strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-category is an
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{A}
such that for every
x\in\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}
there exists a (unique) degree
0
morphism
e_{x}\in\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,x)
, called a strict unit of
x
,
satisfying the following properties:

(1)

m_{2}(-\otimes e_{x})=\operatorname{Id}_{\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y)}
and
m_{2}(e_{x}\otimes-)=\operatorname{Id}_{\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(y,x)}
for every
x,y\in\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}
,

(2)

m_{i}(f_{i}\otimes\ldots\otimes f_{1})=0
if
i\neq 2
and
f_{j}=e_{x}
for some
j\in\{1,\ldots i\}
and some
x\in\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}
.

A
DG
-category can be considered as a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-algebra, because
DG
-categories are required by definition to have units to ensure that they are actually categories.

An
A_{\infty}
-category is called cohomologically unital if
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
is a category, in the sense that
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
has units. A strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{A}
is in particular cohomologically unital.

For an
A_{\infty}
-functor
F\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
we denote by
F_{0}
the morphism of sets
F_{0}\colon\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}\to\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{B}
, and by
F_{i}
(
i\geq 1
) the components of
F
. We write
\textnormal{H}(F)\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\to\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{B})
for the functor induced by
F_{0},F_{1}
between the cohomology categories.

Definition 8.2
.

Let
F\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
be an
A_{\infty}
-functor.

(1)

F
is cohomologically unital if
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}
are cohomologically unital and
\textnormal{H}(F)
is unital.

(2)

F
is strictly unital if
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}
are strictly unital and

•

F_{1}(e_{x})=e_{F_{0}(x)}
for every
x\in\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}
;

•

F_{i}(f_{i}\otimes\ldots\otimes f_{1})=0
if
i\geq 2
and
f_{j}=e_{x}
for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,i\}
and some
x\in\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}
.

(3)

F
is a quasi-isomorphism if
F_{0}\colon\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}\to\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{B}
is a bijection, and for every
x,y\in\operatorname{Ob}\mathcal{A}
,
F_{1}\colon\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y)\to\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}(F_{0}(x),F_{0}(y))
is a quasi-isomorphism.

From now on, for reasons discussed in
[
S2
]
and in
[
COS2
]
, to ensure the existence of a strictly unital minimal model, we require our
DG
- and
A_{\infty}
-categories to satisfy the following property.

Property 8.3
.

For all objects
x
, the complex
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,x)
has non-trivial cohomology.

This property is clearly preserved by quasi-isomorphisms.
Notice that if
\mathcal{A}
is a
DG
-category, or a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-category,
8.3
is equivalent to requiring that for all objects
x
the identity map
e_{x}
is non-trivial in the cohomology of the complex
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,x)
.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-category such that
8.3
holds. Then there exists a strictly unital minimal model for
\mathcal{A}
, i.e., a strictly unital and minimal
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
equipped with two strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms
G\colon\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}
and
F\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Following
[
S1
, 1.13]
, for every pair
x,y
of objects of
\mathcal{A}
it is possible to choose a splitting of the complex
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y)
into a minimal part and an acyclic part, which is isomorphic to
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y))
, and to construct a contracting homotopy
T_{1}
:

\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y))
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y)

\scriptstyle G_{1}
\scriptstyle F_{1}
\scriptstyle T_{1}

Furthermore, by the hypotheses on
\mathcal{A}
, it is possible to choose the splitting in such a way that when
x=y
the chain maps
F_{1}
and
G_{1}
are unit-preserving. Then by
[
S1
, 1.12]
, one can equip the category
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
with an
A_{\infty}
-structure
\{\upmu_{k}\}_{k\geq 2}
and extend
F_{1}
and
G_{1}
to
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms
F\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\upmu_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})
and
G\colon(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\upmu_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}
. Since
\mathcal{A}
is strictly unital,
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
is a cohomologically unital
A_{\infty}
-category, and it is easy to see that because of how we chose
F_{1}
and
G_{1}
, the functors
F,G
are cohomologically unital (for this, see also
[
COS2
, 1.14]
).

The
A_{\infty}
-category
(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\upmu_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})
satisfies the corrected hypotheses of
[
S2
]
, so by
[
S1
, 2.1]
there is a formal diffeomorphism

\upphi\colon(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\upmu_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})\rightsquigarrow(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\uplambda_{k}\}_{k\geq 2}),

where
\{\uplambda_{k}\}_{k\geq 2}
is a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-structure on
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
, induced by
\upphi
. Note that by the proof of
[
S1
, 2.1]
,
(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\uplambda_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})
is still minimal. The formal diffeomorphism
\upphi
above is such that its linear component is the identity, hence it is cohomologically unital.

It follows that there is a diagram of
A_{\infty}
-functors

\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\upmu_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\upphi}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\uplambda_{k}\}_{k\geq 2}),

where both
F
and
\upphi
are cohomologically unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms, both
\mathcal{A}
and
(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\uplambda_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})
are strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-categories, whilst
(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\upmu_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})
is cohomologically unital.

A cohomologically unital functor
F\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
between strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-categories which satisfy
8.3
can be replaced with a strictly unital functor
F^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
homotopic to
F
, by
[
S1
, 2.2]
,
[
LH
, 3.2.2.1, 3.2.4.3, Section 5.1]
, or
[
COS1
, 2.5]
together with
[
COS2
, p. 6]
. Using this, we can construct a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
F^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A}),\{\uplambda_{k}\}_{k\geq 2})
. The same can be done to construct a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
G^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Construction 8.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
DG
-category such that
8.3
holds. Viewing
\mathcal{A}
as a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-category, consider its strictly unital minimal model
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
, as in
8.4
.

(1)

For objects
x\neq y
in
\mathcal{A}
, consider the graded subspace

\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y))\subseteq\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,y)).

(2)

For every object
x\in\mathcal{A}
, consider the graded subspace

\mathbb{C}e_{x}\oplus\textnormal{H}^{>0}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,x))\subseteq\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(x,x)).

Together, (
1
) and (
2
) defines a vector subspace
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
of
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
.

Lemma 8.6
.

The strictly unital, minimal
A_{\infty}
-structure on
\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
restricts to a minimal
A_{\infty}
-structure on
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}

Proof.

This is exactly the same as
5.5
, with the point being that
A_{\infty}
-products on inputs of degree
\geq 1
all clearly land within
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Products which involve degree zero input necessarily involve the units, which by inspection also always land in
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

Definition 8.7
.

We call
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
the
unitally positive
A_{\infty}
-category
associated to
\mathcal{A}
.

Remark 8.8
.

Similarly to
5.7
, we can also prove that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
is well-defined: if
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are quasi-isomorphic

DG
-categories such that
8.3
holds, we can replace the zigzag of quasi-isomorphisms of
DG
-categories between
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
with a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
F\colon\mathcal{A}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{B}
.
We then obtain an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between their strictly unital minimal models constructed in
8.4
, which by a simple degree argument, as in
5.7
, restricts to an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between

\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}
.

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
E_{i},E^{\prime}_{i}
be bounded complexes of projective
\Upgamma
-modules, for
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, with quasi-isomorphisms
g_{i}\colon E_{i}\to E^{\prime}_{i}
for every
i
. Then the
DG
-categories

(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i},E_{j})
and
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E^{\prime}_{i},E^{\prime}_{j})
are quasi-isomorphic.

Proof.

This is entirely based on
[
ST
, 4.4]
, but for completeness we spell out how it works for
DG
-categories with
m
objects.

For every
i
, denote
C_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{cone}(g_{i})=(E_{i}[1]\oplus E_{i}^{\prime},d_{C_{i}})
, and define the
DG
-category with
m
objects:

(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(C_{i},C_{j})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}[1],E_{j}[1])&\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}^{\prime},E_{j}[1])\\
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}[1],E_{j}^{\prime})&\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}^{\prime},E_{j}^{\prime})\end{array}\right)

with obvious differential, composition and identities.
The calculation of
[
ST
, 4.4]
shows that lower triangular matrices are closed for the differentials, so that

(i,j)\mapsto\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}[1],E_{j}[1])&0\\
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}[1],E_{j}^{\prime})&\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}^{\prime},E_{j}^{\prime})\end{array}\right)

with same differentials, composition and identities, is also a
DG
-category with
m
objects. Now following the rest of the proof of
[
ST
, 4.4]
, projections onto the categories

(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}[1],E_{j}[1])\cong\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i},E_{j})

and

(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\Upgamma}(E_{i}^{\prime},E_{j}^{\prime})

give a roof of quasi-isomorphisms of
DG
-categories.
∎

8.2.
The case of idempotents

Let
\mathrm{A},\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}},i^{*}
and
i_{*}
be as in Section
6
.

Setup 8.10
.

Let
S_{1},\ldots,S_{t}\in\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}
be finite dimensional simple modules, and suppose that there exists for every
j\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
a complex of finitely generated projective
\mathrm{A}
-modules

\mathcal{Q}^{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\quad 0\rightarrow Q^{j}_{n-1}\rightarrow Q^{j}_{n-2}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow Q^{j}_{1}\rightarrow Q^{j}_{0}\rightarrow 0

which gives an
\mathrm{A}
-projective resolution of
S_{j}
, such that
\mathrm{P}^{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}i^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{j}
satisfies

\mathrm{H}^{-k}(\mathrm{P}^{j})\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i^{*}S_{j}&\text{ if $k\in\{0,n-1\}$ }\\
0&\text{ otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and hence is a length
n
periodic projective resolution of
S_{j}
.

Under Setup
8.10
, set
\mathcal{A}
to be the
DG
-category with
t
objects
\{1,\ldots,t\}
defined as

(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(i,j)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathrm{P}^{j})

with the obvious composition and identities.
Considering
S_{1},\ldots,S_{t}
as
\mathrm{A}
-modules, we can also consider the
DG
-category
\mathcal{B}
, also with
t
objects, defined as

(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}(i,j)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{j}).

If we choose another set of
\mathrm{A}
-projective resolutions
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime j}\to S_{j}
, it is clear that we obtain another
DG
-category
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
which is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathcal{B}
, by
8.9
. Checking that
\mathcal{A}
is well-defined up to quasi-isomorphism is the same, with one extra step: it relies on the fact that each
S_{j}
is simple, so by
6.4

\mathrm{P}^{j}
and
\mathrm{P}^{\prime j}
are quasi-isomorphic, and so we can also use
8.9
. From this, by
8.8
the
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism class of the
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
is well defined.

Theorem 8.11
.

Under Setup
8.10
, and with notation as above, there is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between the
DG
-category
\mathcal{B}
and the unitally positive
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
associated to
\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

Since for every
j
,
\mathcal{Q}^{j}\to S_{j}
is an
\mathrm{A}
-projective resolution, we have

\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{B})}(i,j)=\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}(i,j))=\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{j}))\cong\textnormal{Ext}^{*}_{\mathrm{A}}(S_{i},S_{j}).

For the
DG
-category
\mathcal{A}
, we can adapt the cohomology calculations of Section
3.3
to see that for
i\neq j
, the only non-zero cohomology for
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathrm{P}^{j})
is

\textnormal{H}^{k}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathrm{P}^{j}))=\begin{cases}\textnormal{Ext}^{k+n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{i},S_{j})&\quad k=1-n,\ldots,0\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{i},S_{j})&\quad k=1,\ldots,n-1\\
\end{cases}

(8.A)

whilst for
i=j
, the only non-zero cohomology is

\textnormal{H}^{k}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{j},\mathrm{P}^{j}))=\begin{cases}\textnormal{Ext}^{k+n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})&\quad k=1-n,\ldots,-1\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})\oplus\mathbb{C}\oplus\operatorname{Im}(\circ d_{0}^{j})&\quad k=0\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})&\quad k=1,\ldots,n-2\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})\oplus\operatorname{Im}(\circ d_{0}^{j})&\quad k=n-1,\end{cases}

(8.B)

where we are using the fact that
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})\cong\mathbb{C}
,
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{i},S_{j})=0
for all
i\neq j
, and the notation
\operatorname{Im}(\circ d_{0}^{j})
refers to the image of the map

\circ d_{0}^{j}\colon\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(P_{n-1}^{j},S_{j})\to\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(P_{0}^{j},S_{j})

considered in
3.7
. Thus the graded components of the unitally positive
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
associated to
\mathcal{A}
of
8.5
are

\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{k}(j,j)=\begin{cases}0&\quad k\leq-1,\ k\geq n\\
\mathbb{C}&\quad k=0\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})&\quad k=1,\ldots,n-2\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})\oplus\operatorname{Im}(\circ d_{0}^{j})&\quad k=n-1.\end{cases}

(8.C)

whilst for
i\neq j
the graded components are

\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{k}(i,j)=\begin{cases}0&\quad k\leq 0,\ i\geq n\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{i},S_{j})&\quad k=1,\ldots,n-1.\end{cases}

(8.D)

Extension of scalars gives a morphism of
DG
-categories
i^{*}\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A}
sending

f\in\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{A}(\mathcal{Q}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{j})=\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{B}}(i,j)\mapsto i^{*}f\in\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathrm{P}^{j})=\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(i,j)

which is the identity on objects. The following commutative diagram is a generalisation of (
6.A
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathrm{P}^{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathcal{P}^{j})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i},S_{j})\cong\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},S_{j})},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upvarepsilon_{j}\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upiota_{j}\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upalpha_{j}\circ}

(8.E)

and the maps
\upvarepsilon_{j}\circ
,
\upalpha_{j}\circ
are still quasi-isomorphisms. This implies

(1)

The morphism of
DG
-categories
i^{*}\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A}
is injective in cohomology.

(2)

The cohomology of
\mathcal{B}
can be calculated explictly, by adapting the cohomology calculation in §
3.3
, namely

\displaystyle\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}}(S_{j},S_{j})

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{H}^{k}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{j},\mathcal{Q}^{j}))

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{H}^{k}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{j},\mathcal{P}^{j}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\quad k<0,\ k\geq n\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})&\quad k=0,\ldots,n-2\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{n-1}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{j},S_{j})\oplus\operatorname{Im}(\circ d_{0}^{j})&\quad i=n-1\end{cases}

and if
i\neq j
then

\displaystyle\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}}(S_{i},S_{j})

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{H}^{k}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{j}))

\displaystyle\cong\textnormal{H}^{k}(\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathcal{P}^{j}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\quad k\leq 0,\ k\geq n\\
\textnormal{Ext}^{k}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{i},S_{j})&\quad k=1,\ldots,n-1.\end{cases}

By comparing this with (
8.C
) and (
8.D
), we can see that for every
i,j
,
\textnormal{Ext}^{*}_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(S_{i},S_{j})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}}(i,j)
are isomorphic as graded vector spaces.

Passing to strictly unital minimal models defined in
8.4
, the morphism of
DG
-categories
i^{*}\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A}
induces a strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-morphism of strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-categories
F\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{B})\rightsquigarrow\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
, such that for every
i,j
its linear component

F_{1}\colon\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{B})}(i,j)\to\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{A})}(i,j)

is injective. By the same argument of
6.6
,
F
restricts to an
A_{\infty}
-morphism
F\colon\textnormal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{B})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}
, such that its linear component is injective. Counting dimensions, the linear component is thus an isomorphism, proving the claim.
∎

The following is the analogue of
6.7
.

Corollary 8.12
.

Suppose that both
\mathrm{A}
and
\mathrm{B}
admit
t
finite dimensional simples, and idempotents which satisfy the conditions in Setup
8.10
, via complexes
\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{A}}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{B}}
of the same length, for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
. If
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
, and each has precisely
t
simple modules, then there is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between the
DG
-categories
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{A}})
and
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i}_{\mathrm{B}},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{B}})
.

Proof.

The simple
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
-modules
S_{j}
are equipped with length
n
complexes
\mathrm{P}^{j}
which build
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{A}}
, and by
8.11
there is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
between the
DG
-category
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{A}})
and the
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{A}}
, equipped with the
A_{\infty}
-structure of
8.6
.
The same holds for
\mathrm{B}
, where
\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
has
t
simples
S^{\prime j}
, equipped with complexes
\mathrm{P}^{\prime j}
of same length
n
, which in turn build
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{B}}
. Again by
8.11
, there is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between the
DG
-category
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i}_{\mathrm{B}},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{B}})
and the
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{B}}
, equipped with the
A_{\infty}
-structure of
8.6
.

It now suffices to prove that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{A}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{B}}
are
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphic, and for this we will ultimately use the fact that the unitally positive category is well-defined up to quasi-isomorphism.
Indeed, since
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\cong\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
, there is an (exact) isomorphism of categories
F\colon\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}\to\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
. Now
F
preserves simples, so without loss of generality we can set
FS^{j}\cong S^{\prime j}
. Further,
F
takes
\mathrm{P}^{j}
to some length
n
complex of projectives which builds a periodic projective resolution of
S^{\prime j}
. Clearly this isomorphism of module categories induces an isomorphism of
DG
-categories

[(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathrm{P}^{j})]\xrightarrow{\cong}\ [(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(F\mathrm{P}^{i},F\mathrm{P}^{j})].

(8.F)

Now for every
j
,
F\mathrm{P}^{j}
is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathrm{P}^{\prime j}
by
6.4
, as both give periodic resolutions of the same simple with the same length. By
8.9
, the
DG
-categories
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(F\mathrm{P}^{i},F\mathrm{P}^{j})
and
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime i},\mathrm{P}^{\prime j})
are then quasi-isomorphic.

Combining this with
(
8.F
), it follows that the
DG
-categories
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{i},\mathrm{P}^{j})
and
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}}(\mathrm{P}^{\prime i},\mathrm{P}^{\prime j})
are quasi-isomorphic. By
8.8
,
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{A}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{B}}
are
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphic, proving the result.
∎

8.3.
General Donovan–Wemyss

We revert to the general flops setup of §
7.1
, where there are
t
curves
C_{1},\ldots,C_{t}
.

Theorem 8.13
.

Suppose that
X_{1}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{1}
and
X_{2}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{2}
are two
3
-fold flopping contractions, where both
X_{i}
are smooth, and both
R_{i}
are complete local. Denote their corresponding contraction algebras by
\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
and
\mathrm{C}_{\textnormal{con}}
, respectively. Then the following conditions are equivalent.

(1)

R_{1}\cong R_{2}
.

(2)

\mathop{\rm{D}^{b}}\nolimits(\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}})\simeq\mathop{\rm{D}^{b}}\nolimits(\mathop{\textnormal{mod}}\nolimits\mathrm{C}_{\textnormal{con}})
.

(3)

\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is isomorphic to an iterated mutation of
\mathrm{C}_{\textnormal{con}}
.

(4)

\mathrm{A}_{\textnormal{con}}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{B}_{\textnormal{con}}
, for some other crepant resolution
Y_{2}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{2}
.

Proof.

(1)
\Rightarrow
(2)
\Leftrightarrow
(3)
\Leftrightarrow
(4) can all be found in the PhD thesis of August
[
A
, 1.4, 1.5]
. The content in the theorem is the direction (4)
\Rightarrow
(1). As in
7.2
, write
\mathrm{A}
and
\mathrm{B}
for the NCCRs corresponding to the two contractions. For both contractions, (
7.B
) now falls within the remit of Setup
8.10
, we and so can simply quote
8.12
to conclude that there must be an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between the
DG
-categories
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i}_{\mathrm{A}},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{A}})
and
(i,j)\mapsto\mathop{\mathcal{H}\rm om}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}^{i}_{\mathrm{B}},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\mathrm{B}})
.

From here, the proof is similar to that of
7.2
: as a consequence of the above,
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{A}}^{j})
and
\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{B}}^{j})
are quasi-isomorphic, when viewed as
DG
-algebras over
K=\mathbb{C}\times\ldots\times\mathbb{C}
. Thus taking their Koszul dual over
K
, it follows that
\Upgamma_{\mathrm{A}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{A}})^{!}
and
\Upgamma_{\mathrm{B}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathop{\mathcal{E}\rm nd}\nolimits_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{B}})^{!}
are
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphic. But these are the derived deformation algebras of Hua-Keller
[
HK
, 2.2]
(see also
[
KY
, 2.6, 2.7]
), and so the derived deformation algebras are
DG
-quasi-isomorphic (see e.g.
[
L
, 2.8]
).

We now simply follow the final five lines of the proof of
[
HK
, 5.11]
, as the quasi-isomorphism between the derived deformation algebras implies that there is an algebraic equivalence between the categories
per(\Upgamma_{\mathrm{A}})
and
per(\Upgamma_{\mathrm{B}})
, and thus between their subcategories with finite dimensional cohomology, since those can be characterised intrinsically. It follows that their quotients, which are called the cluster categories in
[
HK
]
, are also algebraically equivalent, and so by
[
HK
, 5.12]
there is an induced algebraic triangle equivalence between the singularity categories
\mathrm{D}_{\mathsf{sg}}(R_{1})
and
\mathrm{D}_{\mathsf{sg}}(R_{2})
. Thus we obtain an algebra isomorphism
\mathrm{HH}^{0}(\mathrm{D}_{\mathsf{sg}}(R_{1}))\cong\mathrm{HH}^{0}(\mathrm{D}_{\mathsf{sg}}(R_{2}))
, and so by the recovery theorem
[
HK
, 5.9]
, necessarily
R_{1}\cong R_{2}
.
∎
